{\mathcal{C}}
is closed, we have a
{\mathcal{V}}
-adjunction
a\otimes-\dashv_{\mathcal{V}}[a,-]
between
{\mathcal{V}}
-functors
{\mathcal{C}}\to{\mathcal{C}}
.
(Notice that
{\mathcal{C}}^{\mathcal{V}}
is obviously closed.)
This means there is a family of isomorphims
\theta_{b,c}\in{\mathcal{V}}({\mathcal{C}}(ab\to c)\to{\mathcal{C}}(b\to[a,c]))
satisfying (
2.7
) and (
2.8
).
Now we can understand the adjunction (
6.3
):
starting with
g\in{\mathcal{C}}^{\mathcal{V}}(a{\mathcal{F}}(v)\to b)
, its mate is given by

(\eta_{v}(g\circ\theta_{{\mathcal{F}}(v),b}^{a}))\circ(-\circ_{\mathcal{C}}-)\circ\kappa_{1,b}^{a}
.

We now state the essential lemma, whose proof is now easier and more conceptual than the proof of
[
MP17
, Lem. 4.6 and Appendix B]
!

Lemma 6.12
.

The mate of
\mathbf{1}_{a{\mathcal{F}}(v)}
under Adjunction (
6.3
) is
(j_{a}\eta_{v})\circ(-\otimes_{\mathcal{C}}-)
.

Proof.

Setting
b=a{\mathcal{F}}(v)
, we see that the mate of
j_{a{\mathcal{F}}(v)}=(j_{a}j_{{\mathcal{F}}(v)})\circ(-\otimes_{\mathcal{C}}-)
is given by

\displaystyle(\eta_{v}

\displaystyle[(j_{a}j_{{\mathcal{F}}(v)})\circ(-\otimes_{\mathcal{C}}-)\circ\theta_{{\mathcal{F}}(v),a{\mathcal{F}}(v)}])\circ(-\circ_{\mathcal{C}}-)\circ\kappa_{1_{\mathcal{C}},a{\mathcal{F}}(v)}

\displaystyle=([(j_{a}\eta_{v})\circ(-\otimes_{\mathcal{C}}-)][(j_{a}j_{{\mathcal{F}}(v)})\circ(-\otimes_{\mathcal{C}}-)\circ\theta_{{\mathcal{F}}(v),a{\mathcal{F}}(v)}\circ\kappa_{{\mathcal{F}}(v),a{\mathcal{F}}(v)}])\circ(-\circ_{\mathcal{C}}-)

by (
2.7
)

\displaystyle=([(j_{a}\eta_{v})\circ(-\otimes_{\mathcal{C}}-)][(j_{a}j_{{\mathcal{F}}(v)})\circ(-\otimes_{\mathcal{C}}-)])\circ(-\circ_{\mathcal{C}}-)

\displaystyle=(j_{a}[(\eta_{v}j_{{\mathcal{F}}(v)})\circ(-\circ_{\mathcal{C}}-)])\circ(-\otimes_{\mathcal{C}}-)

by (
6.1
)

\displaystyle=(j_{a}\eta_{v})\circ(-\otimes_{\mathcal{C}}-).

Remark 6.13
.

This lemma has many important implications.
First, one can use this lemma and the braided exchange relation to prove that

(\eta_{a,u}^{\mathcal{C}}\eta_{a{\mathcal{F}}(u),v}^{\mathcal{C}})\circ(-\circ_{\mathcal{C}}-)=(\eta_{1_{\mathcal{C}},u}^{\mathcal{C}}\eta_{{\mathcal{F}}(u),v}^{\mathcal{C}})\circ(-\circ_{\mathcal{C}}-)\circ(j_{a}\otimes_{\mathcal{C}}-)=(\eta_{1_{\mathcal{C}},u}^{\mathcal{C}}\eta_{1_{\mathcal{C}},v}^{\mathcal{C}})\circ(-\otimes_{\mathcal{C}}-)\circ(j_{a}\otimes_{\mathcal{C}}-).

These maps above are thus all equal to the mate of
\alpha_{a,u,v}
from (
3.2
) where the right
{\mathcal{V}}
-module structure of
{\mathcal{C}}
is given by
a\vartriangleleft u:=a{\mathcal{F}}(u)
.
This also implies that
\alpha_{a,u,v}=\mathbf{1}_{a}\nu_{u,v}
for all
a\in{\mathcal{C}}
and
u,v\in{\mathcal{V}}
.
Setting
a=1_{\mathcal{C}}
, we have
\nu_{u,v}=\alpha_{1_{\mathcal{C}},u,v}
.
We will use these facts heavily in the proof of Lemma
7.2
below.

With Lemma
6.12
in hand, we can lift
{\mathcal{F}}:{\mathcal{V}}\to{\mathcal{C}}^{\mathcal{V}}
to a braided oplax monoidal functor
{\mathcal{F}}^{\scriptstyle Z}:{\mathcal{V}}\to Z({\mathcal{C}}^{\mathcal{V}})
by defining a half-braiding
e_{a,{\mathcal{F}}(v)}\in{\mathcal{C}}^{\mathcal{V}}(a{\mathcal{F}}(v)\to{\mathcal{F}}(v)a)
to be the mate of
(\eta_{v}j_{a})\circ(-\otimes_{\mathcal{C}}-)
under Adjunction (
6.3
).
Indeed, all proofs in
[
MP17
, §4 and 5]
now apply verbatim to the closed (rather than rigid) case, using Lemma
6.12
rather than
[
MP17
, Lem. 4.6]
.

In the other direction, starting with a closed monoidal category
{\mathcal{T}}
and a strongly unital braided oplax monoidal functor
({\mathcal{F}}^{\scriptscriptstyle Z},\nu):{\mathcal{V}}\to Z({\mathcal{T}})
such that
{\mathcal{F}}:={\mathcal{F}}^{\scriptscriptstyle Z}\circ R
admits a right adjoint
{\mathcal{R}}
(where
R:Z({\mathcal{T}})\to{\mathcal{T}}
is the forgetful functor), we notice that the functors
{\mathcal{L}}_{a}:{\mathcal{V}}\to{\mathcal{T}}
by
{\mathcal{L}}_{a}(v)=a{\mathcal{F}}(v)
admit right adjoints:

(6.4)

\begin{split}{\mathcal{T}}({\mathcal{L}}_{a}(v)\to b)={\mathcal{T}}(a{\mathcal{F}}(v)\to b)\cong{\mathcal{T}}({\mathcal{F}}(v)\to[a,b])\cong{\mathcal{V}}(v\to{\mathcal{R}}([a,b]))\end{split}

We thus define a
{\mathcal{V}}
-category
{\mathcal{T}}_{{\mathbin{/\mkern-6.0mu/}}{\mathcal{F}}}
as follows:

•

The objects of
{\mathcal{T}}_{{\mathbin{/\mkern-6.0mu/}}{\mathcal{F}}}
are the same as those of
{\mathcal{T}}
,

•

The hom objects are given by
{\mathcal{T}}_{{\mathbin{/\mkern-6.0mu/}}{\mathcal{F}}}(a\to b)={\mathcal{R}}([a,b])
.

•

the identity morphism

j_{a}\in{\mathcal{V}}(1_{\mathcal{V}}\to{\mathcal{R}}([a,a]))
is the mate of
\mathbf{1}_{a}\in{\mathcal{T}}(a\to a)
,

•

Using the notation
6
6

6

In
[
MP17
, Def. 6.2]
, we used the notation
[a\to b]:={\mathcal{F}}({\mathcal{T}}_{{\mathbin{/\mkern-6.0mu/}}{\mathcal{F}}}(a\to b))
.
However, now that we use
[a,-]
for the right
{\mathcal{V}}
-adjoint of
a\otimes-
for
a\in{\mathcal{T}}_{{\mathbin{/\mkern-6.0mu/}}{\mathcal{F}}}
, we use
\langle a\to b\rangle
to not overload the bracket notation.

\displaystyle\langle a\to b\rangle

\displaystyle:={\mathcal{F}}({\mathcal{T}}_{{\mathbin{/\mkern-6.0mu/}}{\mathcal{F}}}(a\to b))\text{ and}

\displaystyle\langle a\to b;b\to c;\cdots\rangle

\displaystyle:={\mathcal{F}}({\mathcal{T}}_{{\mathbin{/\mkern-6.0mu/}}{\mathcal{F}}}(a\to b){\mathcal{T}}_{{\mathbin{/\mkern-6.0mu/}}{\mathcal{F}}}(b\to c)\cdots),

we let
\varepsilon^{{\mathcal{T}}_{{\mathbin{/\mkern-6.0mu/}}{\mathcal{F}}}}_{a\to b}\in{\mathcal{T}}(a\langle a\to b\rangle\to b)
be the counit of Adjunction (
6.4
).
The composition
-\circ_{{\mathcal{T}}_{{\mathbin{/\mkern-6.0mu/}}{\mathcal{F}}}}-\in{\mathcal{V}}({\mathcal{T}}_{{\mathbin{/\mkern-6.0mu/}}{\mathcal{F}}}(a\to b){\mathcal{T}}_{{\mathbin{/\mkern-6.0mu/}}{\mathcal{F}}}(b\to c)\to{\mathcal{T}}_{{\mathbin{/\mkern-6.0mu/}}{\mathcal{F}}}(a\to c))
is the mate of

(\mathbf{1}_{a}\nu_{{\mathcal{T}}_{{\mathbin{/\mkern-6.0mu/}}{\mathcal{F}}}(a\to b),{\mathcal{T}}_{{\mathbin{/\mkern-6.0mu/}}{\mathcal{F}}}(b\to c)})\circ(\varepsilon^{{\mathcal{T}}_{{\mathbin{/\mkern-6.0mu/}}{\mathcal{F}}}}_{a\to b}\mathbf{1}_{\langle b\to c\rangle})\circ\varepsilon^{\mathcal{T}}_{b\to c}
.

•

The tensor product
-\otimes_{{\mathcal{T}}_{{\mathbin{/\mkern-6.0mu/}}{\mathcal{F}}}}-\in{\mathcal{V}}({\mathcal{T}}_{{\mathbin{/\mkern-6.0mu/}}{\mathcal{F}}}(a\to b){\mathcal{T}}_{{\mathbin{/\mkern-6.0mu/}}{\mathcal{F}}}(c\to d)\to{\mathcal{T}}_{{\mathbin{/\mkern-6.0mu/}}{\mathcal{F}}}(ac\to bd))
is the mate of

(\mathbf{1}_{ac}\nu_{{\mathcal{T}}_{{\mathbin{/\mkern-6.0mu/}}{\mathcal{F}}}(a\to b),{\mathcal{T}}_{{\mathbin{/\mkern-6.0mu/}}{\mathcal{F}}}(c\to d)})\circ(\mathbf{1}_{a}e_{c,\langle a\to b\rangle}\mathbf{1}_{\langle c\to d\rangle})\circ(\varepsilon^{{\mathcal{T}}_{{\mathbin{/\mkern-6.0mu/}}{\mathcal{F}}}}_{a\to b}\varepsilon^{{\mathcal{T}}_{{\mathbin{/\mkern-6.0mu/}}{\mathcal{F}}}}_{c\to d})
.

The verification that
{\mathcal{T}}_{{\mathbin{/\mkern-6.0mu/}}{\mathcal{F}}}
is a
{\mathcal{V}}
-monoidal category is identical to
[
MP17
, §6.1-6.4]
, as is the monoidal equivalence
{\mathcal{T}}\cong{\mathcal{T}}_{{\mathbin{/\mkern-6.0mu/}}{\mathcal{F}}}^{\mathcal{V}}
.
Clearly
{\mathcal{R}}_{1_{{\mathcal{T}}_{{\mathbin{/\mkern-6.0mu/}}{\mathcal{F}}}}}
admits a left adjoint, as

{\mathcal{V}}(v\to{\mathcal{R}}_{1_{{\mathcal{T}}_{{\mathbin{/\mkern-6.0mu/}}{\mathcal{F}}}}}(a))={\mathcal{V}}(v\to{\mathcal{R}}([1_{\mathcal{T}},a]))\cong{\mathcal{V}}({\mathcal{F}}(v)\to[1_{\mathcal{T}},a])\cong{\mathcal{T}}({\mathcal{F}}(v)\to a)\cong{\mathcal{T}}_{{\mathbin{/\mkern-6.0mu/}}{\mathcal{F}}}({\mathcal{F}}(v)\to a).

It remains to show that
{\mathcal{T}}_{{\mathbin{/\mkern-6.0mu/}}{\mathcal{F}}}
is closed.
First, we can describe the right oplax
{\mathcal{V}}
-module structure of
{\mathcal{T}}_{{\mathbin{/\mkern-6.0mu/}}{\mathcal{F}}}^{\mathcal{V}}
in more detail.

Remark 6.14
.

Since the hom objects of
{\mathcal{T}}_{{\mathbin{/\mkern-6.0mu/}}{\mathcal{F}}}^{\mathcal{V}}
are defined using Adjunction (
6.4
), under the monoidal equivalence
{\mathcal{T}}\cong{\mathcal{T}}_{{\mathbin{/\mkern-6.0mu/}}{\mathcal{F}}}^{\mathcal{V}}
, we may identify the oplax right
{\mathcal{V}}
-module structure by

a\vartriangleleft v=a{\mathcal{F}}(v)
and
\alpha_{a,u,v}=\mathbf{1}_{a}\nu_{u,v}
as in Remark
6.13
.

Recall that by Example
6.5
, for each
a\in{\mathcal{T}}_{{\mathbin{/\mkern-6.0mu/}}{\mathcal{F}}}
, we have a
{\mathcal{V}}
-monoidal functor
a\otimes-:{\mathcal{T}}_{{\mathbin{/\mkern-6.0mu/}}{\mathcal{F}}}\to{\mathcal{T}}_{{\mathbin{/\mkern-6.0mu/}}{\mathcal{F}}}
whose underlying functor is
a\otimes-:{\mathcal{T}}\to{\mathcal{T}}
.

Definition 6.15
.

We denote by
\varepsilon^{\mathcal{T}}
the counit of the adjunction
{\mathcal{T}}(ab\to c)\cong{\mathcal{T}}(b\to[a,c])
, which should not be confused with
\varepsilon^{{\mathcal{T}}_{{\mathbin{/\mkern-6.0mu/}}{\mathcal{F}}}}
!
We define
[a,-]_{b\to c}
to be the mate of
(\varepsilon^{\mathcal{T}}_{a\to b}\mathbf{1}_{\langle b\to c\rangle})\circ\varepsilon^{{\mathcal{T}}_{{\mathbin{/\mkern-6.0mu/}}{\mathcal{F}}}}_{b\to c}
under the adjunction

(6.5)

\begin{split}{\mathcal{V}}({\mathcal{T}}_{{\mathbin{/\mkern-6.0mu/}}{\mathcal{F}}}(b\to c)\to{\mathcal{T}}_{{\mathbin{/\mkern-6.0mu/}}{\mathcal{F}}}([a,b]\to[a,c]))&={\mathcal{V}}({\mathcal{T}}_{{\mathbin{/\mkern-6.0mu/}}{\mathcal{F}}}(b\to c)\to{\mathcal{R}}([[a,b],[a,c]]))\\
&\cong{\mathcal{T}}([a,b]\langle b\to c\rangle\to[a,c])\\
&\cong{\mathcal{T}}(a[a,b]\langle b\to c\rangle\to c).\end{split}

To verify
[a,-]
is a
{\mathcal{V}}
-functor, we calculate that the mates of
([a,-]_{b\to c}[a,-]_{c\to d})\circ(-\circ_{{\mathcal{T}}_{{\mathbin{/\mkern-6.0mu/}}{\mathcal{F}}}}-)
and

(-\circ_{{\mathcal{T}}_{{\mathbin{/\mkern-6.0mu/}}{\mathcal{F}}}}-)\circ[a,-]_{b\to d}

agree under Adjunction (
6.5
).
The mate of the former is given by

\displaystyle(\mathbf{1}_{a}\mathbf{1}_{[a,b]}({\mathcal{F}}([a,-]_{b\to c}[a,-]_{c\to d})

\displaystyle\circ\nu_{{\mathcal{T}}_{{\mathbin{/\mkern-6.0mu/}}{\mathcal{F}}}([a,b]\to[a,c]),{\mathcal{T}}_{{\mathbin{/\mkern-6.0mu/}}{\mathcal{F}}}([a,c]\to[a,d])}))

\displaystyle\circ(\mathbf{1}_{a}\varepsilon^{{\mathcal{T}}_{{\mathbin{/\mkern-6.0mu/}}{\mathcal{F}}}}_{[a,b]\to[a,c]}\mathbf{1}_{\langle[a,c]\to[a,d]\rangle})\circ(\mathbf{1}_{a}\varepsilon^{{\mathcal{T}}_{{\mathbin{/\mkern-6.0mu/}}{\mathcal{F}}}}_{[a,c]\to[a,d]})\circ\varepsilon^{\mathcal{T}}_{a\to d}

\displaystyle=(\mathbf{1}_{a}\mathbf{1}_{[a,b]}\nu_{{\mathcal{T}}_{{\mathbin{/\mkern-6.0mu/}}{\mathcal{F}}}(b\to c),{\mathcal{T}}_{{\mathbin{/\mkern-6.0mu/}}{\mathcal{F}}}(c\to d)})\circ(\mathbf{1}_{a}\mathbf{1}_{[a,b]}{\mathcal{F}}([a,-]_{b\to c}){\mathcal{F}}([a,-]_{c\to d}))

\displaystyle\hskip 85.35826pt\circ(\mathbf{1}_{a}\varepsilon^{{\mathcal{T}}_{{\mathbin{/\mkern-6.0mu/}}{\mathcal{F}}}}_{[a,b]\to[a,c]}\mathbf{1}_{\langle[a,c]\to[a,d]\rangle})\circ(\mathbf{1}_{a}\varepsilon^{{\mathcal{T}}_{{\mathbin{/\mkern-6.0mu/}}{\mathcal{F}}}}_{[a,c]\to[a,d]})\circ\varepsilon^{\mathcal{T}}_{a\to d}

\displaystyle=(\mathbf{1}_{a}\mathbf{1}_{[a,b]}\nu_{{\mathcal{T}}_{{\mathbin{/\mkern-6.0mu/}}{\mathcal{F}}}(b\to c),{\mathcal{T}}_{{\mathbin{/\mkern-6.0mu/}}{\mathcal{F}}}(c\to d)})\circ(\mathbf{1}_{a}\mathbf{1}_{[a,b]}{\mathcal{F}}([a,-]_{b\to c})\mathbf{1}_{\langle c\to d\rangle})

\displaystyle\hskip 85.35826pt\circ(\mathbf{1}_{a}\varepsilon^{{\mathcal{T}}_{{\mathbin{/\mkern-6.0mu/}}{\mathcal{F}}}}_{[a,b]\to[a,c]}\mathbf{1}_{\langle c\to d\rangle})\circ(\varepsilon^{{\mathcal{T}}}_{a\to c}\mathbf{1}_{\langle c\to d\rangle})\circ\varepsilon^{{\mathcal{T}}_{{\mathbin{/\mkern-6.0mu/}}{\mathcal{F}}}}_{c\to d}

\displaystyle=(\varepsilon^{{\mathcal{T}}}_{a\to b}\nu_{{\mathcal{T}}_{{\mathbin{/\mkern-6.0mu/}}{\mathcal{F}}}(b\to c),{\mathcal{T}}_{{\mathbin{/\mkern-6.0mu/}}{\mathcal{F}}}(c\to d)})\circ(\varepsilon^{{\mathcal{T}}_{{\mathbin{/\mkern-6.0mu/}}{\mathcal{F}}}}_{b\to c}\mathbf{1}_{\langle c\to d\rangle})\circ\varepsilon^{{\mathcal{T}}_{{\mathbin{/\mkern-6.0mu/}}{\mathcal{F}}}}_{c\to d}

\displaystyle=(\varepsilon^{{\mathcal{T}}}_{a\to b}{\mathcal{F}}(-\circ_{{\mathcal{T}}_{{\mathbin{/\mkern-6.0mu/}}{\mathcal{F}}}}-))\circ\varepsilon^{{\mathcal{T}}_{{\mathbin{/\mkern-6.0mu/}}{\mathcal{F}}}}_{b\to d},

which is exactly the mate of the latter.

Lemma 6.16
.

The underlying functor
(a\otimes-)^{\mathcal{V}}
is left adjoint to
[a,-]^{\mathcal{V}}
.

Proof.

Under the equivalence
{\mathcal{T}}\cong{\mathcal{T}}_{{\mathbin{/\mkern-6.0mu/}}{\mathcal{F}}}^{\mathcal{V}}
, the underlying functor
(a\otimes-)^{\mathcal{V}}
is just
a\otimes-:{\mathcal{T}}\to{\mathcal{T}}
, and similarly for
[a,-]^{\mathcal{V}}
.
Since
{\mathcal{T}}
is closed, we are finished.
∎

Proposition 6.17
.

The
{\mathcal{V}}
-functor
a\otimes-
is left
{\mathcal{V}}
-adjoint to
[a,-]
.

Proof.

By Lemma
6.16
and Theorem
4.5
, it suffices to prove that
a\otimes-:{\mathcal{T}}_{{\mathbin{/\mkern-6.0mu/}}{\mathcal{F}}}\to{\mathcal{T}}_{{\mathbin{/\mkern-6.0mu/}}{\mathcal{F}}}
is tensored.
For
b\in{\mathcal{T}}_{{\mathbin{/\mkern-6.0mu/}}{\mathcal{F}}}
and
v\in{\mathcal{V}}
,
\mu^{a\otimes-}_{b,v}\in{\mathcal{T}}_{{\mathbin{/\mkern-6.0mu/}}{\mathcal{F}}}^{\mathcal{V}}(ab\vartriangleleft v\to(a\vartriangleleft v)b)={\mathcal{T}}(ab{\mathcal{F}}(v)\to a{\mathcal{F}}(v)b)
is given by the mate of
\eta^{{\mathcal{T}}_{{\mathbin{/\mkern-6.0mu/}}{\mathcal{F}}}}_{b,v}\circ(a\otimes-)_{b\to b\vartriangleleft v}
under the adjunction

{\mathcal{T}}_{{\mathbin{/\mkern-6.0mu/}}{\mathcal{F}}}^{\mathcal{V}}(ab\vartriangleleft v\to a(b\vartriangleleft v))\cong{\mathcal{V}}(v\to{\mathcal{T}}_{{\mathbin{/\mkern-6.0mu/}}{\mathcal{F}}}(ab\to a(b\vartriangleleft v))).

By Remark
6.14
, identifying
{\mathcal{T}}_{{\mathbin{/\mkern-6.0mu/}}{\mathcal{F}}}^{\mathcal{V}}={\mathcal{T}}
identifies
a\vartriangleleft u=a{\mathcal{F}}(u)
and

{\mathcal{T}}_{{\mathbin{/\mkern-6.0mu/}}{\mathcal{F}}}^{\mathcal{V}}(ab\vartriangleleft v\to a(b\vartriangleleft v))={\mathcal{T}}(ab{\mathcal{F}}(v)\to ab{\mathcal{F}}(v))
.
We claim that under this identification,
\mu^{a\otimes-}_{b,v}=\mathbf{1}_{ab{\mathcal{F}}(v)}
, which is obviously invertible.
Indeed, by Example
6.5
,

\eta^{{\mathcal{T}}_{{\mathbin{/\mkern-6.0mu/}}{\mathcal{F}}}}_{b,v}\circ(a\otimes-)_{b\to b\vartriangleleft v}=\eta_{b,v}^{{\mathcal{T}}_{{\mathbin{/\mkern-6.0mu/}}{\mathcal{F}}}}\circ(j_{a}\mathbf{1}_{{\mathcal{T}}_{{\mathbin{/\mkern-6.0mu/}}{\mathcal{F}}}(b\to b{\mathcal{F}}(v))})\circ(-\otimes_{{\mathcal{T}}_{{\mathbin{/\mkern-6.0mu/}}{\mathcal{F}}}}-),

whose mate under the above adjunction is given by

\displaystyle(\mathbf{1}_{ab}{\mathcal{F}}(\eta_{b,v}^{{\mathcal{T}}_{{\mathbin{/\mkern-6.0mu/}}{\mathcal{F}}}}

\displaystyle\circ(j_{a}\mathbf{1}_{{\mathcal{T}}_{{\mathbin{/\mkern-6.0mu/}}{\mathcal{F}}}(b\to b{\mathcal{F}}(v))})))\circ(\mathbf{1}_{ab}\nu_{{\mathcal{T}}_{{\mathbin{/\mkern-6.0mu/}}{\mathcal{F}}}(a\to a),{\mathcal{T}}_{{\mathbin{/\mkern-6.0mu/}}{\mathcal{F}}}(b\to b{\mathcal{F}}(v))})\circ(\mathbf{1}_{a}e_{b,\langle a\to a\rangle}\mathbf{1}_{\langle b\to b{\mathcal{F}}(v)\rangle})\circ(\varepsilon^{{\mathcal{T}}_{{\mathbin{/\mkern-6.0mu/}}{\mathcal{F}}}}_{a\to a}\varepsilon^{{\mathcal{T}}_{{\mathbin{/\mkern-6.0mu/}}{\mathcal{F}}}}_{b\to b{\mathcal{F}}(v)})

\displaystyle=(\mathbf{1}_{ab}[{\mathcal{F}}(\eta_{b,v}^{{\mathcal{T}}_{{\mathbin{/\mkern-6.0mu/}}{\mathcal{F}}}})\circ(j_{{\mathcal{F}}(a)}\mathbf{1}_{{\mathcal{T}}_{{\mathbin{/\mkern-6.0mu/}}{\mathcal{F}}}(b\to b{\mathcal{F}}(v))})])\circ(\mathbf{1}_{a}e_{b,\langle a\to a\rangle}\mathbf{1}_{\langle b\to b{\mathcal{F}}(v)\rangle})\circ(\varepsilon^{{\mathcal{T}}_{{\mathbin{/\mkern-6.0mu/}}{\mathcal{F}}}}_{a\to a}\varepsilon^{{\mathcal{T}}_{{\mathbin{/\mkern-6.0mu/}}{\mathcal{F}}}}_{b\to b{\mathcal{F}}(v)})

\displaystyle=[(\mathbf{1}_{a}j_{{\mathcal{F}}(a)})\circ\varepsilon^{{\mathcal{T}}_{{\mathbin{/\mkern-6.0mu/}}{\mathcal{F}}}}_{a\to a}][(\mathbf{1}_{b}{\mathcal{F}}(\eta_{b,v}^{{\mathcal{T}}_{{\mathbin{/\mkern-6.0mu/}}{\mathcal{F}}}}))\circ\varepsilon^{{\mathcal{T}}_{{\mathbin{/\mkern-6.0mu/}}{\mathcal{F}}}}_{b\to b{\mathcal{F}}(v)}]

\displaystyle=\mathbf{1}_{a}\mathbf{1}_{b{\mathcal{F}}(v)}.

This completes the proof.
∎

Finally,
[
MP17
, §7]
remains unchanged.
This concludes the proof of Theorem
1.1
.
∎

7.
Tensored
{\mathcal{V}}
-monoidal categories

In this section, we assume that
{\mathcal{V}}
is a braided closed monoidal category so that we may form the
{\mathcal{V}}
-monoidal category
\widehat{{\mathcal{V}}}
as in Example
6.2
.

7.1.
Tensored
{\mathcal{V}}
-monoidal categories

Definition 7.1
.

Similar to an ordinary
{\mathcal{V}}
-category, we call a
{\mathcal{V}}
-monoidal category
tensored
if the
{\mathcal{V}}
-representable functors
{\mathcal{R}}^{a}={\mathcal{C}}(a\to-):{\mathcal{C}}\to\widehat{{\mathcal{V}}}
admit left
{\mathcal{V}}
-adjoints.

Suppose
{\mathcal{C}}
is closed and
{\mathcal{R}}^{1_{\mathcal{C}}}={\mathcal{C}}(1_{\mathcal{C}}\to-):{\mathcal{C}}\to\widehat{{\mathcal{V}}}
admits a left
{\mathcal{V}}
-adjoint
{\mathcal{F}}
.
By Remark
2.19
, the underlying functor
{\mathcal{F}}^{\mathcal{V}}:{\mathcal{V}}\to{\mathcal{C}}^{\mathcal{V}}
is left adjoint to the underlying functor
{\mathcal{R}}_{1_{\mathcal{C}}}
.
As in the proof of Theorem
1.1
, every representable functor
{\mathcal{R}}_{a}={\mathcal{C}}(a\to-)^{\mathcal{V}}:{\mathcal{C}}^{\mathcal{V}}\to{\mathcal{V}}
admits a left adjoint
{\mathcal{L}}_{a}
.
This allows us to canonically equip
{\mathcal{F}}^{\mathcal{V}}
with the structure of a strongly unital braided oplax monoidal functor
(({\mathcal{F}}^{\mathcal{V}})^{\scriptscriptstyle Z},\nu):{\mathcal{V}}\to Z({\mathcal{C}}^{\mathcal{V}})
.
In fact, we will see that
(({\mathcal{F}}^{\mathcal{V}})^{\scriptscriptstyle Z},\nu)
is strong monoidal.

Lemma 7.2
.

Suppose
{\mathcal{C}}
is closed and
{\mathcal{R}}^{1_{\mathcal{C}}}={\mathcal{C}}(1_{\mathcal{C}}\to-):{\mathcal{C}}\to\widehat{{\mathcal{V}}}
admits a left
{\mathcal{V}}
-adjoint
{\mathcal{F}}:\widehat{{\mathcal{V}}}\to{\mathcal{C}}
.

(1)

{\mathcal{F}}
is tensored with
(\mu^{\mathcal{F}}_{u,v})^{-1}=\nu_{u,v}
for all
u,v\in\widehat{{\mathcal{V}}}
.

(2)

(({\mathcal{F}}^{\mathcal{V}})^{\scriptscriptstyle Z},\nu):{\mathcal{V}}\to Z({\mathcal{C}}^{\mathcal{V}})
is strong monoidal.

(3)

The morphisms
\nu_{u,v}\in{\mathcal{C}}^{\mathcal{V}}({\mathcal{F}}(uv)\to{\mathcal{F}}(u){\mathcal{F}}(v))
endow
{\mathcal{F}}
with the structure of a
{\mathcal{V}}
-monoidal functor.

(4)

{\mathcal{C}}
is tensored.

Proof.

First,
{\mathcal{F}}
is tensored by Theorem
4.5
.
As explained in Remark
6.13
, notice that
\nu_{u,v}=\alpha_{1_{\mathcal{C}},u,v}
where the right
{\mathcal{V}}
-module structure of
{\mathcal{C}}
is given by
a\vartriangleleft u:=a{\mathcal{F}}(u)
.
Now when
{\mathcal{F}}
is a left
{\mathcal{V}}
-adjoint of
{\mathcal{C}}(1_{\mathcal{C}}\to-)
, we have
\alpha_{1_{\mathcal{C}},u,v}
is invertible with inverse equal to
\mu^{{\mathcal{L}}^{1_{\mathcal{C}}}}_{u,v}=\mu^{\mathcal{F}}_{u,v}
by setting
a=1_{\mathcal{C}}
in the proof of Proposition
4.8
.

By (1),
\nu_{u,v}\in{\mathcal{V}}(1_{\mathcal{V}}\to{\mathcal{C}}({\mathcal{F}}(uv)\to{\mathcal{F}}(u){\mathcal{F}}(v)))
is invertible for all
u,v\in{\mathcal{V}}
, and the result follows immediately.

Since
({\mathcal{F}}^{\mathcal{V}},\nu)
is strong monoidal by (1), we see that the
\nu_{u,v}
automatically satisfy the unitality and associativity axioms for
({\mathcal{F}},\nu)
to be a
{\mathcal{V}}
-monoidal functor, since these are merely properties of the underlying functor
{\mathcal{F}}^{\mathcal{V}}
.
It remains to prove the naturality condition (
6.2
).
Under the adjunction

{\mathcal{V}}(\widehat{{\mathcal{V}}}(u\to w)\widehat{{\mathcal{V}}}(v\to x)\to\widehat{{\mathcal{V}}}({\mathcal{F}}(uv)\to{\mathcal{F}}(w){\mathcal{F}}(x)))\cong{\mathcal{C}}^{\mathcal{V}}({\mathcal{F}}(uv)\widehat{{\mathcal{V}}}(u\to w)\widehat{{\mathcal{V}}}(v\to x)\to{\mathcal{F}}(w){\mathcal{F}}(x)),

the mate of

({\mathcal{F}}_{u\to w}{\mathcal{F}}_{v\to x})\circ(-\otimes_{{\mathcal{C}}}-)\circ(\nu_{u,v}\circ_{{\mathcal{C}}}-)

is given by

\displaystyle(\mathbf{1}_{{\mathcal{F}}(uv)}

\displaystyle[{\mathcal{F}}(({\mathcal{F}}_{u\to w}{\mathcal{F}}_{v\to x})\circ(-\otimes_{{\mathcal{C}}}-)\circ(\nu_{u,v}\circ_{{\mathcal{C}}}-))])

\displaystyle\hskip 79.6678pt\circ(\varepsilon^{\mathcal{C}}_{{\mathcal{F}}(uv)\to{\mathcal{F}}(u){\mathcal{F}}(v)}\mathbf{1}_{{\mathcal{C}}({\mathcal{F}}(u){\mathcal{F}}(v)\to{\mathcal{F}}(w){\mathcal{F}}(x))})\circ\varepsilon^{\mathcal{C}}_{{\mathcal{F}}(u){\mathcal{F}}(v)\to{\mathcal{F}}(w){\mathcal{F}}(x)}

\displaystyle=(\nu_{u,v}{\mathcal{F}}({\mathcal{F}}_{u\to w}{\mathcal{F}}_{v\to x}))\circ(\mathbf{1}_{{\mathcal{F}}(u){\mathcal{F}}(v)}\nu_{\widehat{{\mathcal{V}}}(u\to w),\widehat{{\mathcal{V}}}(v\to x)})\circ(\mathbf{1}_{{\mathcal{F}}(u)}e_{{\mathcal{F}}(v),\langle u\to w\rangle}\mathbf{1}_{\langle v\to x\rangle})\circ(\varepsilon^{\mathcal{C}}_{{\mathcal{F}}(u)\to{\mathcal{F}}(w)}\varepsilon^{\mathcal{C}}_{{\mathcal{F}}(v)\to{\mathcal{F}}(x)})

\displaystyle=(\nu_{u,v}\nu_{\widehat{{\mathcal{V}}}(u\to w),\widehat{{\mathcal{V}}}(v\to x)})\circ(\mathbf{1}_{{\mathcal{F}}(u)}e_{{\mathcal{F}}(v),\langle u\to w\rangle}\mathbf{1}_{\langle v\to x\rangle})

\displaystyle\hskip 91.04872pt\circ([(\mathbf{1}_{{\mathcal{F}}(u)}{\mathcal{F}}({\mathcal{F}}_{u\to w}))\circ\varepsilon^{\mathcal{C}}_{{\mathcal{F}}(u)\to{\mathcal{F}}(w)}][(\mathbf{1}_{{\mathcal{F}}(v)}{\mathcal{F}}({\mathcal{F}}_{v\to x}))\circ\varepsilon^{\mathcal{C}}_{{\mathcal{F}}(v)\to{\mathcal{F}}(x)}])

\displaystyle=(\nu_{u,v}\nu_{\widehat{{\mathcal{V}}}(u\to w),\widehat{{\mathcal{V}}}(v\to x)})\circ(\mathbf{1}_{{\mathcal{F}}(u)}e_{{\mathcal{F}}(v),\langle u\to w\rangle}\mathbf{1}_{\langle v\to x\rangle})

\displaystyle\hskip 91.04872pt\circ([\nu^{-1}_{{\mathcal{F}}(u),\widehat{{\mathcal{V}}}(u\to w)}\circ{\mathcal{F}}(\varepsilon^{\widehat{{\mathcal{V}}}}_{u\to w})][\nu^{-1}_{{\mathcal{F}}(v),\widehat{{\mathcal{V}}}(v\to x)}\circ{\mathcal{F}}(\varepsilon^{\widehat{{\mathcal{V}}}}_{v\to x})])

\displaystyle=\nu^{-1}_{uv,\widehat{{\mathcal{V}}}(u\to w)\widehat{{\mathcal{V}}}(v\to x)}\circ{\mathcal{F}}((\mathbf{1}_{u}\beta_{v,\widehat{{\mathcal{V}}}(u\to w)}\mathbf{1}_{\widehat{{\mathcal{V}}}(v\to x)})\circ(\varepsilon^{\widehat{{\mathcal{V}}}}_{u\to w}\varepsilon^{\widehat{{\mathcal{V}}}}_{v\to x}))\circ\nu_{v,x},

which is also equal to the mate of
(-\otimes_{\widehat{{\mathcal{V}}}}-)\circ{\mathcal{F}}_{uv\to wx}\circ(-\circ_{\mathcal{C}}\nu_{w,x})
through a similar calculation.
Here, we have used that

(7.1)

(\mathbf{1}_{{\mathcal{F}}(u)}{\mathcal{F}}({\mathcal{F}}_{u\to w}))\circ\varepsilon^{\mathcal{C}}_{{\mathcal{F}}(u)\to{\mathcal{F}}(w)}=\nu^{-1}_{u,\widehat{{\mathcal{V}}}(u\to w)}\circ{\mathcal{F}}(\varepsilon^{\widehat{{\mathcal{V}}}}_{u\to w})

and the analogous statement replacing
u
and
w
with
v
and
x
respectively.
Indeed, since
\nu_{u,\widehat{{\mathcal{V}}}(u\to w)}^{-1}=\mu^{\mathcal{F}}_{u,\widehat{{\mathcal{V}}}(u\to w)}
by Proposition
4.8
as in (1), both maps in (
7.1
) are the mate of
{\mathcal{F}}_{u\to w}
under the adjunction

{\mathcal{C}}^{\mathcal{V}}({\mathcal{F}}(u){\mathcal{F}}(\widehat{{\mathcal{V}}}(u\to w))\to{\mathcal{F}}(w))\cong{\mathcal{V}}(\widehat{{\mathcal{V}}}(u\to w)\to{\mathcal{C}}({\mathcal{F}}(u)\to{\mathcal{F}}(w))).

Recall from Remark
6.13
that the right oplax
{\mathcal{V}}
-module structure of
{\mathcal{C}}
is given by
a\vartriangleleft u:=a{\mathcal{F}}(u)
, and
\alpha_{a,u,v}=\mathbf{1}_{a}\nu_{u,v}
for all
a\in{\mathcal{C}}
and
u,v\in{\mathcal{V}}
.
Since
\nu_{u,v}
is invertible by (1), we see
\alpha_{a,u,v}
is invertible for every
a\in{\mathcal{C}}
and
u,v\in{\mathcal{V}}
.
Hence
{\mathcal{C}}
is tensored by Proposition
4.10
and Corollary
4.11
.
∎

The following corollary is now immediate.

Corollary 7.3
.

A closed
{\mathcal{V}}
-monoidal category is tensored if and only if
{\mathcal{R}}^{1_{\mathcal{C}}}
admits a left
{\mathcal{V}}
-adjoint.

7.2.
Classification of tensored closed
{\mathcal{V}}
-monoidal categories

We now prove the analog of Theorem
1.5
for
{\mathcal{V}}
-monoidal categories.
Recall that a monoidal functor
({\mathcal{F}},\nu)
is called
strong
if
\nu
is a natural isomorphism.

Theorem

(Theorem
1.2
)
.

Let
{\mathcal{V}}
be a closed monoidal category.
Under Theorem
1.1
, there is a bijective correspondence

\left\{\,\parbox{196.32384pt}{\rm Tensored closed ${\mathcal{V}}$-monoidal categories}\,\right\}\,\,\cong\,\,\left\{\,\parbox{236.15796pt}{\rm Pairs $({\mathcal{T}},{\mathcal{F}}^{\scriptscriptstyle Z})$ with ${\mathcal{T}}$ a closed monoidal category and ${\mathcal{F}}^{\scriptscriptstyle Z}:{\mathcal{V}}\to Z({\mathcal{T}})$ braided strong monoidal, such that ${\mathcal{F}}:={\mathcal{F}}^{\scriptscriptstyle Z}\circ R$ admits a right adjoint}\,\right\}.

We also get a bijective correspondence replacing closed with rigid on both sides above.

Proof.

Under the bijective correspondence from Theorem
1.1
, it suffices to prove that
{\mathcal{C}}
tensored implies
({\mathcal{F}}^{\scriptscriptstyle Z},\nu):{\mathcal{V}}\to Z({\mathcal{C}}^{\mathcal{V}})
is strong monoidal, and that
({\mathcal{F}},\nu):{\mathcal{V}}\to Z({\mathcal{T}})
being strong monoidal implies
{\mathcal{T}}_{{\mathbin{/\mkern-6.0mu/}}{\mathcal{F}}}
is tensored.

First, when
{\mathcal{C}}
is closed and tensored,
{\mathcal{R}}^{1_{\mathcal{C}}}={\mathcal{C}}(1_{\mathcal{C}}\to-):{\mathcal{C}}\to\widehat{{\mathcal{V}}}
admits a left
{\mathcal{V}}
-adjoint
{\mathcal{F}}:{\mathcal{V}}\to\widehat{{\mathcal{C}}}
.
By Remark
2.19
, the underlying functor
{\mathcal{F}}^{\mathcal{V}}
is a left adjoint to the underlying functor
{\mathcal{R}}_{1_{\mathcal{C}}}
, and can be endowed with the structure of a braided oplax monoidal functor
(({\mathcal{F}}^{\mathcal{V}})^{\scriptscriptstyle Z},\nu):{\mathcal{V}}\to Z({\mathcal{C}}^{\mathcal{V}})
.
By Lemma
7.2
, we see that
(({\mathcal{F}}^{\mathcal{V}})^{\scriptscriptstyle Z},\nu)
is strong monoidal.

Conversely, suppose
{\mathcal{F}}^{\scriptscriptstyle Z}:{\mathcal{V}}\to Z({\mathcal{T}})
is strong monoidal.
By (4) of Lemma
7.2
, it suffices to show we can promote
{\mathcal{F}}={\mathcal{F}}^{\scriptscriptstyle Z}\circ R:{\mathcal{V}}\to{\mathcal{T}}
(where
R:Z({\mathcal{T}})\to{\mathcal{T}}
is the forgetful functor) to a
{\mathcal{V}}
-functor
{\mathcal{F}}:\widehat{{\mathcal{V}}}\to{\mathcal{T}}_{{\mathbin{/\mkern-6.0mu/}}{\mathcal{F}}}
which is left
{\mathcal{V}}
-adjoint to
{\mathcal{T}}_{{\mathbin{/\mkern-6.0mu/}}{\mathcal{F}}}(1_{\mathcal{T}}\to-)
.
Since
{\mathcal{F}}
is strong monoidal, we can define
{\mathcal{F}}_{u\to v}
as mate of
\nu^{-1}_{u,\widehat{{\mathcal{V}}}(u\to v)}\circ{\mathcal{F}}(\varepsilon^{\widehat{{\mathcal{V}}}}_{u\to v})

as on the right hand side of (
7.1
)
under the adjunction

{\mathcal{V}}(\widehat{{\mathcal{V}}}(u\to v)\to{\mathcal{T}}_{{\mathbin{/\mkern-6.0mu/}}{\mathcal{F}}}({\mathcal{F}}(u)\to{\mathcal{F}}(v)))\cong{\mathcal{T}}({\mathcal{F}}(u){\mathcal{F}}(\widehat{{\mathcal{V}}}(u\to v))\to{\mathcal{F}}(v)).

Under the adjunction

{\mathcal{V}}(\widehat{{\mathcal{V}}}(u\to v)\widehat{{\mathcal{V}}}(v\to w)\to{\mathcal{T}}_{{\mathbin{/\mkern-6.0mu/}}{\mathcal{F}}}({\mathcal{F}}(u)\to{\mathcal{F}}(w)))\cong{\mathcal{T}}_{{\mathbin{/\mkern-6.0mu/}}{\mathcal{F}}}^{\mathcal{V}}({\mathcal{F}}(u){\mathcal{F}}(\widehat{{\mathcal{V}}}(u\to v)\widehat{{\mathcal{V}}}(v\to w)\to{\mathcal{F}}(u)),

we see the mate of

({\mathcal{F}}_{u\to v}{\mathcal{F}}_{v\to w})\circ(-\circ_{{\mathcal{T}}_{{\mathbin{/\mkern-6.0mu/}}{\mathcal{F}}}}-)

is given by

\displaystyle(\mathbf{1}_{{\mathcal{F}}(u)}{\mathcal{F}}({\mathcal{F}}_{u\to v}{\mathcal{F}}_{v\to w}))\circ(\mathbf{1}_{{\mathcal{F}}(u)}\circ\nu_{{\mathcal{T}}_{{\mathbin{/\mkern-6.0mu/}}{\mathcal{F}}}(u\to v),{\mathcal{T}}_{{\mathbin{/\mkern-6.0mu/}}{\mathcal{F}}}(v\to w)})\circ(\varepsilon^{{\mathcal{T}}_{{\mathbin{/\mkern-6.0mu/}}{\mathcal{F}}}}_{{\mathcal{F}}(u)\to{\mathcal{F}}(v)}\mathbf{1}_{\langle v\to w\rangle})\circ\varepsilon^{{\mathcal{T}}_{{\mathbin{/\mkern-6.0mu/}}{\mathcal{F}}}}_{{\mathcal{F}}(v)\to{\mathcal{F}}(w)}

\displaystyle=(\mathbf{1}_{{\mathcal{F}}(u)}\circ\nu_{\widehat{{\mathcal{V}}}(u\to v),\widehat{{\mathcal{V}}}(v\to w)})\circ([(\mathbf{1}_{{\mathcal{F}}(u)}{\mathcal{F}}({\mathcal{F}}_{u\to v}))\circ\varepsilon^{{\mathcal{T}}_{{\mathbin{/\mkern-6.0mu/}}{\mathcal{F}}}}_{{\mathcal{F}}(u)\to{\mathcal{F}}(v)}]\mathbf{1}_{\langle v\to w\rangle})\circ[(\mathbf{1}_{{\mathcal{F}}(v)}{\mathcal{F}}({\mathcal{F}}_{v\to w}))\circ\varepsilon^{{\mathcal{T}}_{{\mathbin{/\mkern-6.0mu/}}{\mathcal{F}}}}_{{\mathcal{F}}(v)\to{\mathcal{F}}(w)}]

\displaystyle=(\mathbf{1}_{{\mathcal{F}}(u)}\circ\nu_{\widehat{{\mathcal{V}}}(u\to v),\widehat{{\mathcal{V}}}(v\to w)})\circ([\nu^{-1}_{u,\widehat{{\mathcal{V}}}(u\to v)}{\mathcal{F}}(\varepsilon^{\widehat{{\mathcal{V}}}}_{u\to v})]\mathbf{1}_{\langle v\to w\rangle})\circ[\nu^{-1}_{v,\widehat{{\mathcal{V}}}(v\to w)}{\mathcal{F}}(\varepsilon^{\widehat{{\mathcal{V}}}}_{v\to w})]

\displaystyle=\nu^{-1}_{u,\widehat{{\mathcal{V}}}(u\to v)\widehat{{\mathcal{V}}}(v\to w)}\circ{\mathcal{F}}((\varepsilon^{\widehat{{\mathcal{V}}}}_{u\to v}\mathbf{1}_{\widehat{{\mathcal{V}}}(v\to w)})\circ\varepsilon^{\widehat{{\mathcal{V}}}}_{v\to w})

\displaystyle=\nu^{-1}_{u,\widehat{{\mathcal{V}}}(u\to v)\widehat{{\mathcal{V}}}(v\to w)}\circ{\mathcal{F}}((\mathbf{1}_{u}(-\circ_{\widehat{{\mathcal{V}}}}-))\circ\varepsilon^{\widehat{{\mathcal{V}}}}_{u\to w})

\displaystyle=(\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{F}}(u){\mathcal{F}}(-\circ_{\widehat{{\mathcal{V}}}}-))\circ\nu^{-1}_{u,\widehat{{\mathcal{V}}}(u\to w)}\circ{\mathcal{F}}(\varepsilon^{\widehat{{\mathcal{V}}}}_{u\to w})

which is exactly the mate of
(-\circ_{\widehat{{\mathcal{V}}}}-)\circ{\mathcal{F}}_{u\to w}
.
Hence
{\mathcal{F}}
is a
{\mathcal{V}}
-functor.

We already know the underlying functor
{\mathcal{F}}^{\mathcal{V}}:{\mathcal{V}}\to{\mathcal{T}}_{{\mathbin{/\mkern-6.0mu/}}{\mathcal{F}}}^{\mathcal{V}}
is left adjoint to
{\mathcal{R}}_{1_{{\mathcal{T}}_{{\mathbin{/\mkern-6.0mu/}}{\mathcal{F}}}}}:{\mathcal{T}}_{{\mathbin{/\mkern-6.0mu/}}{\mathcal{F}}}^{\mathcal{V}}\to{\mathcal{V}}
by the proof of Theorem
1.1
in §
6.4
.
Thus by Theorem
4.5
, to show
{\mathcal{F}}
is a left
{\mathcal{V}}
-adjoint of
{\mathcal{T}}/\penalty{\mathcal{F}}(1_{{\mathcal{T}}/\penalty{\mathcal{F}}}\to-)
, it suffices to prove that
{\mathcal{F}}
is tensored.
Note that
\mu^{\mathcal{F}}_{u,v}
is by definition the mate of
\eta^{\widehat{{\mathcal{V}}}}_{u,v}\circ{\mathcal{F}}_{u\to uv}
under the adjunction

{\mathcal{T}}_{{\mathbin{/\mkern-6.0mu/}}{\mathcal{F}}}^{\mathcal{V}}({\mathcal{F}}(u){\mathcal{F}}(v)\to{\mathcal{F}}(uv))\cong{\mathcal{V}}(v\to{\mathcal{T}}_{{\mathbin{/\mkern-6.0mu/}}{\mathcal{F}}}({\mathcal{F}}(u)\to{\mathcal{F}}(uv))).

But notice by definition of
{\mathcal{F}}_{u\to uv}
, this mate is also given by

(\mathbf{1}_{{\mathcal{F}}(u)}{\mathcal{F}}(\eta^{\widehat{{\mathcal{V}}}}_{u,v}))\circ\nu^{-1}_{u,\widehat{{\mathcal{V}}}(u\to uv)}\circ{\mathcal{F}}(\varepsilon^{\widehat{{\mathcal{V}}}}_{u\to uv})=\nu^{-1}_{u,v}\circ{\mathcal{F}}((\mathbf{1}_{u}\eta^{\widehat{{\mathcal{V}}}}_{u,v})\circ\varepsilon^{\widehat{{\mathcal{V}}}}_{u\to uv})=\nu^{-1}_{u,v}.

Hence
\mu^{\mathcal{F}}_{u,v}=\nu^{-1}_{u,v}
is invertible, and
{\mathcal{F}}
is tensored.
∎

8.
Completion for
{\mathcal{V}}
-monoidal categories

We now discuss the completion operation for
{\mathcal{V}}
-monoidal categories.
In this section,
{\mathcal{V}}
is braided and closed so we may form the self-enriched
{\mathcal{V}}
-monoidal category
\widehat{{\mathcal{V}}}
.

Definition 8.1
.

Given a
{\mathcal{V}}
-monoidal category
{\mathcal{C}}
, we define its completion
\overline{{\mathcal{C}}}
as an extension of Definition
5.1
.
As before,
\overline{C}
has objects of the form
a\blacktriangleleft u
for
a\in{\mathcal{C}}
and
u\in{\mathcal{V}}
.
We define
\overline{{\mathcal{C}}}(a\blacktriangleleft u\to b\blacktriangleleft v)
,
j_{a\blacktriangleleft u}
, and
-\circ_{\overline{{\mathcal{C}}}}-
as before.
We additionally define:

•

1_{\overline{{\mathcal{C}}}}:=1_{\mathcal{C}}\blacktriangleleft 1_{\mathcal{V}}
.

•

(a\blacktriangleleft u)(b\blacktriangleleft v):=ab\blacktriangleleft uv
.

•

-\otimes_{\overline{{\mathcal{C}}}}-
is the mate of

(\mathbf{1}_{u}\beta_{w,\widehat{{\mathcal{V}}}(u\to{\mathcal{C}}(a\to b)v)}\mathbf{1}_{\widehat{{\mathcal{V}}}(w\to{\mathcal{C}}(c\to d)x)})\circ(\varepsilon^{\widehat{{\mathcal{V}}}}_{u\to{\mathcal{C}}(a\to b)v}\varepsilon^{\widehat{{\mathcal{V}}}}_{w\to{\mathcal{C}}(c\to d)x})\circ(\mathbf{1}_{{\mathcal{C}}(a\to b)}\beta_{v,{\mathcal{C}}(c\to d)}^{-1}\mathbf{1}_{x})\circ((-\otimes_{\mathcal{C}}-)\mathbf{1}_{vx})

under the adjunction

\displaystyle{\mathcal{V}}(\widehat{{\mathcal{V}}}(u\to{\mathcal{C}}(a\to b)v)

\displaystyle\widehat{{\mathcal{V}}}(w\to{\mathcal{C}}(c\to d)x)\to\widehat{{\mathcal{V}}}(uw\to{\mathcal{C}}(ac\to bd)vx)

\displaystyle\cong{\mathcal{V}}(uw\widehat{{\mathcal{V}}}(u\to{\mathcal{C}}(a\to b)v)\widehat{{\mathcal{V}}}(w\to{\mathcal{C}}(c\to d)x)\to{\mathcal{C}}(ac\to bd)vx).

It is a worthwhile exercise to verify that
\overline{{\mathcal{C}}}
satisfies the axioms of a
{\mathcal{V}}
-monoidal category.

Suppose
{\mathcal{C}}
is a
{\mathcal{V}}
-monoidal category, and form
\overline{{\mathcal{C}}}
as above.
Note that
\overline{{\mathcal{C}}}
is tensored by Corollary
5.4
, since this is merely a property of the underlying
{\mathcal{V}}
-category of
\overline{{\mathcal{C}}}
(obtained by forgetting the
{\mathcal{V}}
-monoidal structure).

8.1.
Universal property of completion for
{\mathcal{V}}
-monoidal categories

Definition 8.2
.

We now endow our
{\mathcal{V}}
-functor
{\mathcal{I}}:{\mathcal{C}}\to\overline{{\mathcal{C}}}
by
a\mapsto a\blacktriangleleft 1_{\mathcal{V}}

from Definition
5.5

with the structure of a
{\mathcal{V}}
-monoidal functor.
We define

\nu^{\mathcal{I}}_{a,b}\in{\mathcal{V}}(1_{\mathcal{V}}\to\overline{{\mathcal{C}}}(ab\blacktriangleleft 1_{\mathcal{V}}\to(a\blacktriangleleft 1_{\mathcal{V}})(b\blacktriangleleft 1_{\mathcal{V}})))={\mathcal{V}}(1_{\mathcal{V}}\to\overline{{\mathcal{C}}}(ab\blacktriangleleft 1_{\mathcal{V}}\to ab\blacktriangleleft 1_{\mathcal{V}}))

to be
j_{ab\blacktriangleleft 1_{\mathcal{V}}}
.
It is straightforward to check the necessary diagrams commute, and
({\mathcal{I}},\nu)
is
{\mathcal{V}}
-monoidal.

Theorem 8.3
.

Suppose
{\mathcal{C}}
and
{\mathcal{D}}
are
{\mathcal{V}}
-monoidal categories with
{\mathcal{D}}
tensored
and closed
and
({\mathcal{F}},\nu^{\mathcal{F}}):{\mathcal{C}}\to{\mathcal{D}}
is a
{\mathcal{V}}
-monoidal functor such that the underlying
{\mathcal{V}}
-functor
{\mathcal{F}}:{\mathcal{C}}\to{\mathcal{D}}
is tensored.
There exists a tensored
{\mathcal{V}}
-monoidal functor
(\overline{{\mathcal{F}}},\nu^{\overline{{\mathcal{F}}}}):\overline{{\mathcal{C}}}\to{\mathcal{D}}
such that
{\mathcal{I}}\circ\overline{{\mathcal{F}}}\cong{\mathcal{F}}
as
{\mathcal{V}}
-monoidal functors.

Proof.

By Proposition
5.7
, we know that the underlying
{\mathcal{V}}
-functor of
{\mathcal{F}}
(forgetting the
{\mathcal{V}}
-monoidal structure) factors through a tensored
{\mathcal{V}}
-functor
\overline{{\mathcal{F}}}:\overline{{\mathcal{C}}}\to{\mathcal{D}}
, i.e., there is a
{\mathcal{V}}
-natural isomorphism
\sigma:{\mathcal{F}}\Rightarrow{\mathcal{I}}\circ\overline{{\mathcal{F}}}
.
It remains to show
\overline{{\mathcal{F}}}
can be endowed with the structure of a
{\mathcal{V}}
-monoidal functor such that
\sigma
is
{\mathcal{V}}
-monoidal.

Since
{\mathcal{D}}
is tensored,
under the bijective correspondence in Theorem
1.2
, there is a strong monoidal functor
({\mathcal{G}}^{\scriptscriptstyle Z},\nu^{\mathcal{G}}):{\mathcal{V}}\to Z({\mathcal{D}}^{\mathcal{V}})
, and we define
{\mathcal{G}}:={\mathcal{G}}^{\scriptscriptstyle Z}\circ R
where
R:Z({\mathcal{D}}^{\mathcal{V}})\to{\mathcal{D}}^{\mathcal{V}}
is the forgetful functor.
Moreover, note that the right
{\mathcal{V}}
-module structure of
{\mathcal{D}}^{\mathcal{V}}
is given by
d\vartriangleleft v=d{\mathcal{G}}(v)
with
\alpha_{d,u,v}=\mathbf{1}_{d}\nu^{\mathcal{G}}_{u,v}
similar to Remark
6.13
.
We define
\nu^{\overline{{\mathcal{F}}}}_{a\blacktriangleleft u,b\blacktriangleleft v}
for
a\blacktriangleleft u,b\blacktriangleleft v\in\overline{{\mathcal{C}}}

to be equal to

(\nu^{\mathcal{F}}_{a,b}\nu^{\mathcal{G}}_{u,v})\circ(\mathbf{1}_{{\mathcal{F}}(a)e_{{\mathcal{F}}(b),{\mathcal{G}}(u)}}\mathbf{1}_{{\mathcal{G}}(v)})

in

{\mathcal{D}}^{\mathcal{V}}(\overline{{\mathcal{F}}}((a\blacktriangleleft u)(b\blacktriangleleft v))\to\overline{{\mathcal{F}}}(a\blacktriangleleft u)\overline{{\mathcal{F}}}(b\blacktriangleleft v))={\mathcal{D}}^{\mathcal{V}}({\mathcal{F}}(ab){\mathcal{G}}(uv)\to{\mathcal{F}}(a){\mathcal{G}}(u){\mathcal{F}}(b){\mathcal{G}}(v)).

Here,
e_{{\mathcal{F}}(b),{\mathcal{G}}(u)}
is the half-braiding for
{\mathcal{G}}^{\scriptscriptstyle Z}(u)\in Z({\mathcal{D}}^{\mathcal{V}})
.
Associativity now follows immediately from associativity of
\nu^{\mathcal{F}}
and
\nu^{\mathcal{G}}
, together with the hexagon axiom for the half-braidings
e
, and the fact that each
\nu^{{\mathcal{G}}}
is actually morphism in
Z({\mathcal{D}}^{\mathcal{V}})
.
(Indeed, this proof is similar to the displayed diagram in the proof of
[
HPT16
, Prop. 7.4]
, where all tensorators are implicitly suppressed.)
Moreover, under the identification of the right
{\mathcal{V}}
-module structure
d\vartriangleleft v:=d{\mathcal{G}}(v)
as in Remark
6.13
, we have that

\sigma_{a}:=\rho^{\mathcal{D}}_{a}\in{\mathcal{V}}(1_{\mathcal{V}}\to{\mathcal{D}}({\mathcal{F}}(a)\to{\mathcal{F}}(a)\vartriangleleft 1_{\mathcal{V}}))={\mathcal{V}}(1_{\mathcal{V}}\to{\mathcal{D}}({\mathcal{F}}(a)\to{\mathcal{F}}(a)))

is identified with
j_{{\mathcal{F}}(a)}
, and thus
\sigma
is obviously monoidal.

Finally, to verify (
6.2
), one shows the mates of the mophisms

(\overline{{\mathcal{F}}}_{a\blacktriangleleft u\to c\blacktriangleleft w}\overline{{\mathcal{F}}}_{b\blacktriangleleft v\to d\blacktriangleleft x})\circ(-\otimes_{{\mathcal{D}}}-)\circ(\nu_{a\blacktriangleleft u,b\blacktriangleleft v}^{\overline{{\mathcal{F}}}}\circ_{\mathcal{D}}-)\qquad\text{and}\qquad(-\otimes_{\overline{{\mathcal{C}}}}-)\circ\overline{{\mathcal{F}}}_{ab\blacktriangleleft uv\to cd\blacktriangleleft wx}\circ(-\circ_{\mathcal{D}}\nu_{c\blacktriangleleft w,d\blacktriangleleft x}^{\overline{{\mathcal{F}}}})

are equal under the adjunction

\displaystyle{\mathcal{V}}(

\displaystyle\overline{{\mathcal{C}}}(a\blacktriangleleft u\to c\blacktriangleleft w)\overline{{\mathcal{C}}}(b\blacktriangleleft v\to d\blacktriangleleft x)\to{\mathcal{D}}({\mathcal{F}}(ab)\vartriangleleft uv\to({\mathcal{F}}(c)\vartriangleleft w)({\mathcal{F}}(d)\vartriangleleft x)))

\displaystyle={\mathcal{V}}(\widehat{{\mathcal{V}}}(u\to{\mathcal{C}}(a\to c)w)\widehat{{\mathcal{V}}}(v\to{\mathcal{C}}(b\to d)x)\to{\mathcal{D}}({\mathcal{F}}(ab){\mathcal{G}}(uv)\to{\mathcal{F}}(c){\mathcal{G}}(w){\mathcal{F}}(d){\mathcal{G}}(x)))

\displaystyle\cong{\mathcal{D}}^{\mathcal{V}}({\mathcal{F}}(ab){\mathcal{G}}(uv){\mathcal{G}}(\widehat{{\mathcal{V}}}(u\to{\mathcal{C}}(a\to c)w)\widehat{{\mathcal{V}}}(v\to{\mathcal{C}}(b\to d)x))\to{\mathcal{F}}(c){\mathcal{G}}(w){\mathcal{F}}(d){\mathcal{G}}(x)),

where one uses the definitions of
-\circ_{\mathcal{D}}-
,
\overline{{\mathcal{F}}}
, and
-\otimes_{\overline{{\mathcal{C}}}}-
as mates given in Section
3.2
(see also
[
MP17
, Prop. 4.9]
), Proposition
5.7
, and Definition
8.1
respectively.
We leave this enjoyable exercise to the reader, who may wish to use the string diagrammatic calculus to perform this calculation.
We point out that one should keep in mind that
{\mathcal{G}}
applied to any morphism in
{\mathcal{V}}
is a morphism in
Z({\mathcal{D}}^{\mathcal{V}})
.
∎

8.2.
When tensored
{\mathcal{V}}
-monoidal categories are equivalent to their completions

Since being tensored is a property of the underlying
{\mathcal{V}}
-category of a
{\mathcal{V}}
-monoidal category, we now adapt the results of §
5
to the
{\mathcal{V}}
-monoidal setting by merely checking monoidality when necessary.

Recall from Lemma
5.9
that
\tau_{a\blacktriangleleft u}:=\mu^{\mathcal{I}}_{a,u}
defines a
1_{\mathcal{V}}
-graded
{\mathcal{V}}
-natural transformation
\tau:\mathbf{1}^{\overline{{\mathcal{C}}}}\Rightarrow\overline{\mathbf{1}^{\mathcal{C}}}\circ{\mathcal{I}}
.

Lemma 8.4
.

Suppose
{\mathcal{C}}
is closed.
The
1_{\mathcal{V}}
-graded
{\mathcal{V}}
-natural transformation
\tau:\mathbf{1}^{\overline{{\mathcal{C}}}}\Rightarrow\overline{\mathbf{1}^{\mathcal{C}}}\circ{\mathcal{I}}
is monoidal.

Proof.

We must verify for
a\blacktriangleleft u,b\blacktriangleleft v\in\overline{{\mathcal{C}}}
that the composites

\tau_{ab\blacktriangleleft uv}\circ\nu^{\overline{\mathbf{1}^{\mathcal{C}}}\circ{\mathcal{I}}}_{a\blacktriangleleft u,b\blacktriangleleft v}

and

\nu^{\mathbf{1}^{\overline{{\mathcal{C}}}}}_{a\blacktriangleleft u,b\blacktriangleleft v}\circ(\tau_{a\blacktriangleleft u}\tau_{b\blacktriangleleft v})

are equal in

\displaystyle\overline{{\mathcal{C}}}^{\mathcal{V}}((a\blacktriangleleft u)(b\blacktriangleleft v)\to(a\vartriangleleft u\blacktriangleleft 1_{\mathcal{V}})(b\vartriangleleft v\blacktriangleleft 1_{\mathcal{V}}))

\displaystyle=\overline{{\mathcal{C}}}^{\mathcal{V}}(ab\blacktriangleleft uv\to a{\mathcal{F}}(u)b{\mathcal{F}}(v)\blacktriangleleft 1_{\mathcal{V}})

\displaystyle={\mathcal{V}}(uv\to{\mathcal{C}}(ab\to a{\mathcal{F}}(u)b{\mathcal{F}}(v)))

(8.1)

\displaystyle\cong{\mathcal{C}}^{\mathcal{V}}(ab{\mathcal{F}}(uv)\to a{\mathcal{F}}(u)b{\mathcal{F}}(v))

where
{\mathcal{F}}:{\mathcal{V}}\to Z({\mathcal{C}}^{\mathcal{V}})
is the strong monoidal functor from Theorem
1.2
,
and we have identified
\widehat{{\mathcal{V}}}^{\mathcal{V}}={\mathcal{V}}
as in Example
2.11
.
Under the above Adjunction (
8.1
),
the mate of

\mu^{\mathcal{I}}_{a,u}\mu^{\mathcal{I}}_{b,v}=(\eta^{\mathcal{C}}_{a,u}\eta^{\mathcal{C}}_{b,v})\circ(-\otimes_{\mathcal{C}}-)

is given by

\displaystyle(\mathbf{1}_{ab}{\mathcal{F}}(\eta^{\mathcal{C}}_{a,u}\eta^{\mathcal{C}}_{b,v}))

\displaystyle\circ(\mathbf{1}_{ab}\nu_{{\mathcal{C}}(a\to a{\mathcal{F}}(u)),{\mathcal{C}}(b\to b{\mathcal{F}}(v))})\circ(\mathbf{1}_{a}e_{b,\langle a\to a{\mathcal{F}}(u)\rangle}\mathbf{1}_{\langle b\to b{\mathcal{F}}(v)\rangle})\circ(\varepsilon^{\mathcal{C}}_{a\to a{\mathcal{F}}(u)}\varepsilon^{\mathcal{C}}_{b\to b{\mathcal{F}}(v)})

\displaystyle=(\mathbf{1}_{ab}\nu_{u,v})\circ(\mathbf{1}_{ab}{\mathcal{F}}(\eta^{\mathcal{C}}_{a,u}){\mathcal{F}}(\eta^{\mathcal{C}}_{b,v}))\circ(\mathbf{1}_{a}e_{b,\langle a\to a{\mathcal{F}}(u)\rangle}\mathbf{1}_{\langle b\to b{\mathcal{F}}(v)\rangle})\circ(\varepsilon^{\mathcal{C}}_{a\to a{\mathcal{F}}(u)}\varepsilon^{\mathcal{C}}_{b\to b{\mathcal{F}}(v)})

\displaystyle=(\mathbf{1}_{ab}\nu_{u,v})\circ(\mathbf{1}_{a}e_{b,{\mathcal{F}}(u)}\mathbf{1}_{{\mathcal{F}}(v)})\circ([(\mathbf{1}_{a}{\mathcal{F}}(\eta^{\mathcal{C}}_{a,u}))\circ\varepsilon^{\mathcal{C}}_{a\to a{\mathcal{F}}(u)}][(\mathbf{1}_{b}{\mathcal{F}}(\eta^{\mathcal{C}}_{b,v}))\circ\varepsilon^{\mathcal{C}}_{b\to b{\mathcal{F}}(v)}])

\displaystyle=(\mathbf{1}_{ab}\nu_{u,v})\circ(\mathbf{1}_{a}e_{b,{\mathcal{F}}(u)}\mathbf{1}_{{\mathcal{F}}(v)}),

which is exactly the mate of

\mu_{ab,uv}^{\mathcal{I}}\circ\nu^{\overline{\mathbf{1}^{\mathcal{C}}}\circ{\mathcal{I}}}_{a\blacktriangleleft u,b\blacktriangleleft v}
.
∎

Theorem 8.5
.

The following are equivalent for a tensored closed
{\mathcal{V}}
-monoidal category
{\mathcal{C}}
.

(1)

Every
{\mathcal{V}}
-representable functor
{\mathcal{R}}^{a}={\mathcal{C}}(a\to-):{\mathcal{C}}\to\widehat{{\mathcal{V}}}
is tensored.

(2)

The
{\mathcal{V}}
-monoidal functor
{\mathcal{I}}:{\mathcal{C}}\to\overline{{\mathcal{C}}}
given by
a\mapsto a\blacktriangleleft 1_{\mathcal{V}}
is tensored.

(3)

The
1_{\mathcal{V}}
-graded monoidal
{\mathcal{V}}
-natural transformation
\tau:\mathbf{1}^{\overline{{\mathcal{C}}}}\Rightarrow\overline{\mathbf{1}^{\mathcal{C}}}\circ{\mathcal{I}}
is an isomorphism.

(4)

The
{\mathcal{V}}
-monoidal functors
{\mathcal{I}}:{\mathcal{C}}\to\overline{{\mathcal{C}}}

and

\overline{\mathbf{1}^{\mathcal{C}}}:\overline{{\mathcal{C}}}\to{\mathcal{C}}

witness a
{\mathcal{V}}
-monoidal equivalence.

Proof.

We know the
{\mathcal{V}}
-functors
({\mathcal{I}},\nu^{\mathcal{I}}):{\mathcal{C}}\to\overline{{\mathcal{C}}}
and
(\overline{\mathbf{1}^{\mathcal{C}}},\nu^{\overline{\mathbf{1}^{\mathcal{C}}}}):\overline{{\mathcal{C}}}\to{\mathcal{C}}
are monoidal
and the
1_{\mathcal{V}}
-graded
{\mathcal{V}}
-natural isomorphism
\sigma:\overline{\mathbf{1}^{\mathcal{C}}}\circ{\mathcal{I}}\Rightarrow\mathbf{1}^{\overline{{\mathcal{C}}}}
and the
1_{\mathcal{V}}
-graded
{\mathcal{V}}
-natural transformation
\tau:\mathbf{1}^{\overline{{\mathcal{C}}}}\Rightarrow\overline{\mathbf{1}^{\mathcal{C}}}\circ{\mathcal{I}}
are monoidal.
Hence the result follows formally from Theorem
5.12
.
∎

Combining the above theorem with §
5.4
, when
{\mathcal{V}}
is rigid, we get the following corollaries.

Corollary 8.6
.

Suppose
{\mathcal{V}}
is rigid and
{\mathcal{C}}
is a closed
{\mathcal{V}}
-monoidal category.
Then
\overline{\overline{{\mathcal{C}}}}
is
{\mathcal{V}}
-monoidally equivalent to
\overline{{\mathcal{C}}}
.

Corollary 8.7
.

Suppose
{\mathcal{V}}
is rigid and
{\mathcal{C}}
is a tensored closed
{\mathcal{V}}
-monoidal category.
Then
{\mathcal{C}}
is
{\mathcal{V}}
-monoidally equivalent to
\overline{{\mathcal{C}}}
.

8.3.
Is the completion closed?

In addition to Remark
5.8
, we have the following interesting question.
If
{\mathcal{C}}
is a closed
{\mathcal{V}}
-monoidal category, when is
\overline{{\mathcal{C}}}
closed?
Of course,
\overline{{\mathcal{C}}}
is closed under any of the hypotheses of Theorem
8.5
, as
\overline{{\mathcal{C}}}
is
{\mathcal{V}}
-monoidally equivalent to
{\mathcal{C}}
.
But perhaps
\overline{{\mathcal{C}}}
is closed under some weaker assumptions, e.g., one could additionally assume
{\mathcal{C}}(1_{\mathcal{C}}\to-)
admits either a left adjoint or a left
{\mathcal{V}}
-adjoint.

The problem arises when trying to define the object
[a\blacktriangleleft u,c\blacktriangleleft w]
in a way where we get an underlying adjunction

\overline{{\mathcal{C}}}^{\mathcal{V}}((a\blacktriangleleft u)(b\blacktriangleleft v)\to c\blacktriangleleft w)\cong\overline{{\mathcal{C}}}^{\mathcal{V}}(b\blacktriangleleft v\to[a\blacktriangleleft u,c\blacktriangleleft w]).

Under the identification
\widehat{{\mathcal{V}}}^{\mathcal{V}}={\mathcal{V}}
, the left hand side above is equal to

{\mathcal{V}}(uv\to{\mathcal{C}}(ab\to c)w)
, and at this point it is not clear to us how to proceed unless
{\mathcal{V}}
is rigid.

Example 8.8
.

Building on Example
5.13
, when
{\mathcal{V}}
is rigid, we can describe the
{\mathcal{V}}
-monoidal structure of
\widehat{{\mathcal{V}}}
in more detail without taking mates.
The tensor product morphism is given by

-\otimes_{\widehat{{\mathcal{V}}}}-=\beta^{-1}_{u^{*}w,v^{*}}\mathbf{1}_{x}\in{\mathcal{V}}(\widehat{{\mathcal{V}}}(u\to w)\widehat{{\mathcal{V}}}(v\to x)\to\widehat{{\mathcal{V}}}(uv\to wx))={\mathcal{V}}(u^{*}wv^{*}v\to v^{*}u^{*}wx).

We can describe the
{\mathcal{V}}
-monoidal structure of
\overline{{\mathcal{C}}}
in greater detail without taking mates.
Indeed,
the tensor product morphism is given by

\displaystyle-\otimes_{\overline{{\mathcal{C}}}}-

\displaystyle=(\beta^{-1}_{u^{*}{\mathcal{C}}(a\to c)w,v^{*}}\mathbf{1}_{{\mathcal{C}}(b\to d)x})\circ(\mathbf{1}_{v^{*}u^{*}{\mathcal{C}}(a\to c)}\beta^{-1}_{w,{\mathcal{C}}(b\to d)}\mathbf{1}_{x})\circ(\mathbf{1}_{v^{*}u^{*}}(-\otimes_{\mathcal{C}}-)\mathbf{1}_{wx})

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt\in{\mathcal{V}}(\overline{{\mathcal{C}}}(a\blacktriangleleft u\to c\blacktriangleleft w)\overline{{\mathcal{C}}}(b\blacktriangleleft v\to d\blacktriangleleft x)\to\overline{{\mathcal{C}}}(ab\blacktriangleleft uv\to cd\blacktriangleleft wx))

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt={\mathcal{V}}(u^{*}{\mathcal{C}}(a\to c)wv^{*}{\mathcal{C}}(b\to d)x\to v^{*}u^{*}{\mathcal{C}}(ab\to cd)wx)

Definition 8.9
.

Suppose
{\mathcal{C}}
is a closed
{\mathcal{V}}
-monoidal category and
{\mathcal{V}}
is rigid.
By Lemma
5.14
, every
{\mathcal{V}}
-representable functor
\widehat{{\mathcal{V}}}(u\to-):\widehat{{\mathcal{V}}}\to\widehat{{\mathcal{V}}}
is tensored.
In other words,
\widehat{{\mathcal{V}}}(u\to vw)=u^{*}vw=\widehat{{\mathcal{V}}}(u\to v)w
for all
u,v,w\in{\mathcal{V}}
.

Recall from Example
6.5
that

\displaystyle(a\blacktriangleleft u\otimes-)_{b\blacktriangleleft v\to c\blacktriangleleft w}

\displaystyle:=(j_{a\blacktriangleleft u}\mathbf{1}_{\overline{{\mathcal{C}}}(b\blacktriangleleft v\to c\blacktriangleleft w)})\circ(-\otimes_{\overline{{\mathcal{C}}}}-)

\displaystyle=\mathbf{1}_{v^{*}}[(\coev_{u}\mathbf{1}_{{\mathcal{C}}(b\to c)})\circ\beta^{-1}_{u^{*},{\mathcal{C}}(b\to c)}\circ(j_{a}\otimes_{\mathcal{C}}-)]\mathbf{1}_{w}

gives a well-defined
{\mathcal{V}}
-functor
\overline{{\mathcal{C}}}\to\overline{{\mathcal{C}}}
.
It is easy to verify that setting
[a\blacktriangleleft u,b\blacktriangleleft v]:=[a,b]\blacktriangleleft u^{*}v

and

\displaystyle[a\blacktriangleleft u,-]_{b\blacktriangleleft v\to c\blacktriangleleft w}

\displaystyle=\mathbf{1}_{v^{*}}[(\mathbf{1}_{v^{*}}[a,-]_{b\to c}\mathbf{1}_{w})\circ(\mathbf{1}_{v^{*}}\coev_{u^{*}}\mathbf{1}_{{\mathcal{C}}([a,b]\to[a,c])}\mathbf{1}_{w})\circ(\mathbf{1}_{u^{**}}\beta^{-1}_{u^{*},{\mathcal{C}}([a,b]\to[a,c]})]\mathbf{1}_{w}

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt\in{\mathcal{V}}(v^{*}{\mathcal{C}}(b\to c)w\to v^{*}u^{**}{\mathcal{C}}([a,b]\to[a,c])u^{*}w)

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt={\mathcal{V}}(\overline{{\mathcal{C}}}(b\blacktriangleleft v\to c\blacktriangleleft w)\to\overline{{\mathcal{C}}}([a,b]\blacktriangleleft\widehat{{\mathcal{V}}}(u\to v)\to[a,c]\blacktriangleleft\widehat{{\mathcal{V}}}(u\to w)))

gives a well defined
{\mathcal{V}}
-functor
[a\blacktriangleleft u,-]:\overline{{\mathcal{C}}}\to\overline{{\mathcal{C}}}
.

Proposition 8.10
.

The
{\mathcal{V}}
-functor
((a\blacktriangleleft u)\otimes-)
is left
{\mathcal{V}}
-adjoint to
[a\blacktriangleleft u,-]
via the isomorphism

\displaystyle\overline{\theta}_{b\blacktriangleleft v,c\blacktriangleleft v}

\displaystyle:=\mathbf{1}_{v^{*}}[\beta^{-1}_{u^{*},{\mathcal{C}}(ab\to c)}\circ(\mathbf{1}_{u^{*}}\theta_{b,c})]\mathbf{1}_{w}

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt\in{\mathcal{V}}(v^{*}u^{*}{\mathcal{C}}(ab\to c)w\to v^{*}{\mathcal{C}}(b\to[a,c]u^{*}w),

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt={\mathcal{V}}(\overline{{\mathcal{C}}}((a\blacktriangleleft u)(b\blacktriangleleft v)\to c\blacktriangleleft w)\to\overline{{\mathcal{C}}}(b\blacktriangleleft v\to[a\blacktriangleleft u,c\blacktriangleleft w])),

where
\theta
is the isomorphism for the
{\mathcal{V}}
-adjunction
(a\otimes-)\dashv_{\mathcal{V}}[a,-]
.
Thus
\overline{{\mathcal{C}}}
is closed.

Proof.

First, (
2.7
) holds if and only if

(((a\blacktriangleleft u)\otimes-)_{b\blacktriangleleft v\to d\blacktriangleleft x}\mathbf{1}_{\overline{{\mathcal{C}}}(ad\blacktriangleleft ux\to c\blacktriangleleft w)})\circ(-\circ_{\overline{{\mathcal{C}}}}-)\circ\overline{\theta}_{b\blacktriangleleft v,c\blacktriangleleft w}=(\mathbf{1}_{\overline{{\mathcal{C}}}(b\blacktriangleleft v\to d\blacktriangleleft x)}\overline{\theta}_{d\blacktriangleleft x,c\blacktriangleleft w})\circ(-\circ_{\overline{{\mathcal{C}}}}-).

It is straightforward to check that the above equality follows from the fact that

((j_{a}\otimes_{\mathcal{C}}-)\mathbf{1}_{{\mathcal{C}}(ad\to c)})\circ(-\circ_{\mathcal{C}}-)\circ\theta_{b,c}=(\mathbf{1}_{{\mathcal{C}}(b\to d)}\theta_{d,c})\circ(-\circ_{\mathcal{C}}-),

which is exactly (
2.7
) for
\theta
for the
{\mathcal{V}}
-adjunction
(a\otimes-)\dashv_{\mathcal{V}}[a,-]
.
Similarly, the fact that (
2.8
) holds for
\overline{\theta}
follows immediately from

(-\circ_{\mathcal{C}}-)\circ\theta_{b,d}=(\theta_{b,c}[a,-]_{c\to d})\circ(-\circ_{\mathcal{C}}-),

which is exactly (
2.8
) for
\theta
for the
{\mathcal{V}}
-adjunction
(a\otimes-)\dashv_{\mathcal{V}}[a,-]
.
∎
